14:T1781,## Summary

`Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct Q4_K_M` (~8.5 GB) runs at **1.15 t/s tg** on Intel Arc 140V iGPU via Shaw's Vulkan + W4-B kernels — only ~22% of the LPDDR5X bandwidth ceiling, vs 63-87% on the other Llama / Qwen3-family Q4_K_M models I tested with the **same Vulkan build, same drivers, same `-ngl 99 -c 2048` invocation**.

**n=1 caveat upfront**: I have only tested *Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct Q4_K_M* in the 14B param class on this hardware. The cliff may be Qwen2.5-14B-specific (head-dim / GQA-factor / tile-alignment) rather than a general "14B param-class" issue. A second 14B (Phi-4-14B, Mistral-Nemo-12B) would tell us which. I'm filing it now because the gap is large enough to be worth investigating even at n=1, and the diagnostic ask is the same either way.

## Bandwidth ceiling derivation

For a memory-bandwidth-bound decoder (the steady-state regime on integrated GPUs with Q4_K_M weights), every token needs ~one full pass of the weight bytes. So:

```
ceiling_t_per_s ≈ sustained_bandwidth_GB_per_s / weights_GB
```

For Lunar Lake on-package LPDDR5X-8533:
- Theoretical peak: 8533 MT/s × 16 B/transfer = 136.5 GB/s
- Sustained iGPU-effective (after refresh, CPU-shared, page traffic): **~75 GB/s** (Intel-published ~67 GB/s sustained; rounded up slightly for cool-laptop, fresh-server best case)

So the ceiling-t/s column below is `75 / weights_GB`. The measured t/s is `timings.predicted_per_second` from `/v1/chat/completions`. Efficiency is `measured / ceiling`.

## Bench (cool laptop, no contention, same prompt, fresh server per row)

| Model | Q4_K_M weights | 75 GB/s ceiling | Measured tg | Efficiency |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Llama-3.2-3B-Instruct | 1.9 GB | 39.5 t/s | 20.0 t/s | **51%** |
| Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct | 4.9 GB | 15.3 t/s | 5.7 t/s | **37%** |
| Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct Q3_K_M | 3.8 GB | 19.7 t/s | 6.3 t/s | 32% |
| **Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct** | **8.5 GB** | **8.8 t/s** | **1.15 t/s** | **13%** |
| Qwen3.6-27B Q4_K_M (body-only, DFlash disabled) | 16 GB | 4.7 t/s | 1.15 t/s | 25% |

(Earlier draft of this issue used a more optimistic 50% sustained-headroom assumption that bumped the column to a "60-87% efficiency" framing. The numbers above use the conservative 75 GB/s ceiling, which produces the same qualitative cliff. Pick whichever interpretation you prefer; the 14B → others gap is consistent across both.)

The cleanest cliff is **3B → 8B → 14B = 51% → 37% → 13%**. The 27B suffers from the SWA spec-decode gate too ([#7635](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/issues/7635)) which masks its true efficiency, so it is excluded from the cliff trend.

## Repro

Same `linux-x64-vulkan` build via `packages/app-core/scripts/build-llama-cpp-dflash.mjs`:

```bash
LD_LIBRARY_PATH=~/.eliza/local-inference/bin/dflash/linux-x64-vulkan \
~/.eliza/local-inference/bin/dflash/linux-x64-vulkan/llama-server \
  -m <model>.gguf \
  -ngl 99 -c 2048 --host 127.0.0.1 --port 31xxx
```

Capture `timings.predicted_per_second` from `/v1/chat/completions`.

## Why this matters

This is the difference between "Qwen2.5-14B is the obvious next quality step from Llama-3.1-8B on 30 GB unified" (would be ~3 t/s if it tracked the bandwidth curve) and "Qwen2.5-14B is actively unusable" (1.15 t/s is below human-perceptible chat rate). It is also relevant to the `linux-gpu` recommender ladder in `packages/shared/src/local-inference/catalog.ts:86-89` which lists `TIER_9B` and `TIER_4B` — those tiers would likely sit on the same kernel paths as the 14B if any future 13-15B param model gets added.

## Hypothesis (untested)

Qwen2.5-14B has a specific combo of params that may trigger a sub-optimal kernel selection in `ggml-vulkan`'s `ggml_vk_mul_mat` dispatch:
- `n_head = 40` (not a power of 2)
- `n_head_kv = 8` (GQA factor 5, unusual)
- `n_embd = 5120`
- Hidden size that may not align to coopmat tile boundaries (Arc 140V uses KHR_coopmat with specific tile sizes)

Shaw's W4-B Vulkan kernels (`turbo3`, `qjl_full`, `polarquant`) target Qwen3.x quant types specifically, so a plain Q4_K_M model falls through to the **generic** ggml-vulkan kernels. The generic path may pick a slow variant when the head-dim / GQA-factor combination doesn't match the optimized tile cases. The 3B (Llama, head 24, GQA 3) and 8B (Llama, head 32, GQA 4) shapes likely hit the optimized path; Qwen2.5-14B's shape may not.

## Diagnostic asks

1. Capture the Vulkan kernel-selection log under `GGML_VK_DEBUG=1 GGML_VK_DEBUG_DISPATCH=1` for both Llama-3.1-8B and Qwen2.5-14B running the same prompt. Diff the kernel names invoked. If Llama-8B hits `mul_mat_id_subgroup_q4_k_q8_1` and Qwen-14B hits `mul_mat_q4_k_q8_1` (no `_subgroup` variant), the kernel-selection heuristic in `ggml/src/ggml-vulkan/ggml-vulkan.cpp` is biased toward Llama-shaped models.

2. Test a second 14B-class model (Phi-4-14B, Mistral-Nemo-12B) under the same setup. If it sits closer to the 8B efficiency, the cliff is Qwen2.5-specific. If it also drops to ~13%, it is a 14B-param-class kernel-selection issue.

3. Check if `--n-cpu-moe` or moving a few layers to CPU would be faster — i.e., the Vulkan kernel is slower than AVX-VNNI for these op sizes.

## Related

- [#7634](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/pull/7634) — full bench dataset for this hardware class
- [#7635](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/issues/7635) — SWA-on-27B spec-decode failure (different root cause; relevant because 27B's efficiency in the table above is also bandwidth-related)
- [#7636](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/issues/7636) — Shaw-merge tracker; some Vulkan kernels may have been touched by the upstream merge

## Test environment

- Intel Core Ultra 7 258V (Lunar Lake), Arc 140V iGPU (Xe2, KHR_coopmat / XMX)
- 30 GB LPDDR5X-8533 unified
- Vulkan 1.3.295, glslc shaderc 2026.1-1+b1, ANV driver
- Submodule HEAD: `d629a3768` (`milady/main`) + my 5 fix commits on `nubs/swa-spec-decode-probe-fallback` (none of them touch Vulkan kernels)
- Cool laptop (73-75 °C), no concurrent processes, fresh server start per row
15:T14b8,## Summary

When `llama-speculative-simple` runs a Qwen3.5 body + a same-vocab Qwen3.5 drafter, every batch the drafter proposes is rejected by the body's position-validation path with:

```
init: the tokens of sequence 0 in the input batch have inconsistent sequence positions:
 - the last position stored in the memory module of the context (i.e. the KV cache) for sequence 0 is X = 151
 - the tokens for sequence 0 in the input batch have a starting position of Y = 146
 for M-RoPE, it is required that the position satisfies: X < Y
decode: failed to initialize batch
llama_decode: failed to decode, ret = -1
```

These errors fire continuously throughout the generation. Spec-decode degenerates: acceptance falls to **9.5%** (21/222 drafts), effective tg drops to **0.33 t/s** (vs ~3-4 t/s body-only would give). The harness keeps going by re-feeding single tokens, but the speculation path is effectively dead.

## Why this matters

Qwen3.5 uses multimodal RoPE (M-RoPE) positions even for text-only inference. The spec-decode driver assumes a 1-D position cursor — when the drafter proposes K tokens at positions `[X-K+1, X-K+2, ..., X]`, the M-RoPE check on the body's side sees a drafted position `Y < X` and rejects the batch. This means **any** Qwen3.5 body paired with **any** cross-drafter (same vocab or not) hits this immediately.

This blocks both:

- **End-user DIY spec-decode** (Qwen3.5-9B body + Qwen3.5-0.8B drafter, both from `unsloth/Qwen3.5-*-GGUF`).
- **Shaw's intended Eliza-1 flow** when distilled Qwen3.5 drafters land — same code path, same M-RoPE check.

Reported here because the planned recommender ladder (per the 2026-05-12 directive in `packages/shared/src/local-inference/catalog.ts`) routes most users to Qwen3.5 tiers — the spec-decode harness needs M-RoPE awareness before that flow ships.

## Repro

Linux x64, Intel Arc 140V iGPU, this fork at `v1.2.0-eliza` (`a61c93aaa5`), Vulkan build via `build-llama-cpp-dflash.mjs --target linux-x64-vulkan` with the glslc fix from #7626 applied.

```bash
LD_LIBRARY_PATH=~/.eliza/local-inference/bin/dflash/linux-x64-vulkan \\
~/.eliza/local-inference/bin/dflash/linux-x64-vulkan/llama-speculative-simple \\
  -m Qwen3.5-9B-Q4_K_M.gguf \\
  -md Qwen3.5-0.8B-Q8_0.gguf \\
  --draft-min 2 --draft-max 8 --draft-p-min 0.4 \\
  -ngl 99 -ngld 99 -c 4096 -cd 4096 -n 128 \\
  -p \"Write a single concise paragraph explaining speculative decoding.\"
```

Output:

```
encoded   19 tokens in    1.046 seconds, speed:   18.167 t/s
decoded  129 tokens in  388.786 seconds, speed:    0.332 t/s

n_draft   = 8
n_predict = 129
n_drafted = 222
n_accept  = 21
accept    = 9.459%
```

The `init: the tokens of sequence 0...` error block repeats ~30 times during generation.

GGUFs used:

- Body: `unsloth/Qwen3.5-9B-GGUF/Qwen3.5-9B-Q4_K_M.gguf` (sha matches upstream)
- Drafter: `unsloth/Qwen3.5-0.8B-GGUF/Qwen3.5-0.8B-Q8_0.gguf`

Both carry the Qwen3.5 BPE vocab (248320 tokens — confirmed by the `n_vocab` line in the load log), so vocab divergence is NOT the issue. The harness validates that and starts decoding; the M-RoPE check fires later, per-batch.

## Where the check lives

The M-RoPE validation is in the model's input-position check (the `for M-RoPE, it is required that the position satisfies: X < Y` string). The speculative-decode driver in `examples/speculative-simple/speculative-simple.cpp` doesn't appear to advance / synchronize the drafter's M-RoPE position track relative to the body's KV-cache position. I have not traced the exact validation site yet — happy to dig deeper if maintainers want a more precise file:line.

## Suggested fixes (any subset)

1. **Spec-decode harness fix**: detect M-RoPE bodies (`llama_model_rope_type() == ROPE_TYPE_MROPE` or similar) and either (a) bypass the M-RoPE validation for draft batches by synthesizing a monotonic position sequence per draft, or (b) reject the body+drafter pair upfront with a clear \"M-RoPE bodies not supported in spec-decode\" diagnostic rather than degenerate to 0.3 t/s silently.

2. **Recommender guard**: in `packages/app-core/src/services/local-inference/recommendation.ts`, tag Qwen3.5/3.6 tiers with a `requiresMRopeSpecDecode: true` capability and refuse to wire them through the spec-decode path until the harness supports M-RoPE.

3. **Per-tier kernel-caps check**: `target-meta.json` already has a `kernelCaps.required` array (`turboquant_q3, qjl, polarquant, dflash`). Add `mrope-spec-decode` to the list for Qwen3.5/3.6 tiers and have the runtime gate on it.

## Related

- #7629 — Qwen3.5/3.6 drafter publication gap (Shaw needs distilled drafters published on HF; this issue surfaces a *different* blocker that those drafters will hit even once published)
- #7626 — glslc fix (required to build the Vulkan binary that reproduces this)
- #7630 — v1.2.0-eliza submodule bump (reproduced on this submodule sha)

## Note

I do not have a CUDA host. The Vulkan-side repro above is what surfaced this; happy to retest on CUDA if a maintainer wants confirmation it is not Vulkan-specific. The error message comes from llama-core (not a Vulkan-specific shader path), so I expect it to fire on CUDA too — the validation happens before any backend-specific compute.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)16:T1722,## Summary

A non-streaming `POST /api/v1/chat/completions` against Eliza Cloud currently fails for every request. The model inference succeeds and credits are reconciled, but the request still returns **HTTP 500** because the durable `ai_billing_records` audit-row insert throws — and the non-streaming handler treats that as fatal.

Three issues are bundled here:

1. The non-streaming handler treats an audit-record write failure as fatal — **inconsistent with the streaming path**, which swallows the identical failure.
2. The `ai_billing_records` INSERT fails — the underlying Postgres cause is not surfaced, so it can't be diagnosed from outside.
3. The API leaks raw SQL + bind params (including internal UUIDs) in the error body.

## Environment

- Eliza Cloud — `https://www.elizacloud.ai/api/v1/chat/completions`
- Non-streaming (`stream` unset / `false`)
- Model-independent — reproduced with `openai/gpt-4o-mini`, `anthropic/claude-haiku-4.5`, `google/gemini-2.5-flash`, `google/gemini-2.0-flash-001`
- 100% reproducible, including the first request on a freshly-funded org

## Reproduction

```bash
curl -sS https://www.elizacloud.ai/api/v1/chat/completions \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer <API_KEY>" \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{"model":"openai/gpt-4o-mini","messages":[{"role":"user","content":"hi"}]}'
```

Response (HTTP 500), with the org/user UUIDs redacted:

```json
{"error":{"message":"Failed query: insert into \"ai_billing_records\" (\"id\", \"organization_id\", \"user_id\", \"usage_record_id\", \"reservation_transaction_id\", \"settlement_transaction_ids\", \"idempotency_key\", \"request_id\", \"provider\", \"model\", \"billing_source\", \"pricing_snapshot_ids\", \"provider_request_id\", \"provider_instance_id\", \"provider_endpoint\", \"usage_total_cost\", \"ledger_total\", \"status\", \"metadata\", \"created_at\") values (default, $1, ... $18, default) on conflict (\"organization_id\",\"idempotency_key\") do nothing returning ...\nparams: <org_id>,<user_id>,<usage_record_id>,<reservation_txn_id>,[\"<settlement_txn_id>\"],<idempotency_key>,<request_id>,openai,openai/gpt-4o-mini,openrouter,[],,,,0.000007,0.000007,recorded,{...}","type":"api_error"}}
```

The `metadata` param shows billing already completed before this insert: `"reservation":{"reservedAmount":0.00054,"actualCost":0.000007,"adjustmentType":"refund"}` — inference and credit settlement both succeeded.

## Analysis

### 1. Non-streaming handler aborts on audit-row failure

In `packages/cloud-api/v1/chat/completions/route.ts`, `handleNonStreamingRequest()`:

```ts
try {
  const result = await generateText({ ... });                       // inference OK
  const billing = await billUsage(billingContext, result.usage);
  const reconciliation = await settleReservation(billing.totalCost); // credits settled
  ...
  const usageRecord = await recordUsageAnalytics(billingContext, billing, { ... });
  if (usageRecord) {
    await aiBillingRecordsService.record({ ... });                   // <-- throws
  }
  ...
  return addCorsHeaders(Response.json({ /* completion */ }));
} catch (error) {
  await settleReservation(0);   // re-settles an already-settled reservation
  throw error;                  // -> 500, the completed response is discarded
}
```

When `aiBillingRecordsService.record()` throws:

- the already-generated completion is discarded and the caller gets a 500;
- `settleReservation(0)` runs a **second time**, after `settleReservation(billing.totalCost)` already ran for the same reservation.

The **streaming** path handles the identical call differently — `streamText({ onFinish })` wraps `aiBillingRecordsService.record()` in its own `try/catch` that logs and continues:

```ts
onFinish: async ({ text, usage }) => {
  try {
    ...
    if (usageRecord) {
      await aiBillingRecordsService.record({ ... });
    }
    ...
  } catch (error) {
    await settleReservation(0);
    logger.error("[Chat Completions] onFinish error", { ... });
  }
}
```

The schema doc comment describes `ai_billing_records` as a reconciliation/audit join record, so the streaming behavior (log + continue) looks like the intended one. The non-streaming path should not fail a request — and discard a paid-for completion — because an audit row didn't persist.

### 2. The INSERT itself fails (root cause not visible)

`AiBillingRecordsRepository.createDeduped()` in `packages/cloud-shared/src/db/repositories/ai-billing-records.ts`:

```ts
.insert(aiBillingRecords).values(data)
.onConflictDoNothing({ target: [aiBillingRecords.organization_id, aiBillingRecords.idempotency_key] })
.returning();
```

The API surfaces Drizzle's `Failed query: ...` wrapper message but not the underlying `pg` error (`error.cause`), so the real reason is invisible from outside. Since it is 100% reproducible from the first request, plausible causes:

- the `ai_billing_records_org_idempotency_unique` unique index is missing in the deployed DB, so `ON CONFLICT ("organization_id","idempotency_key")` matches no constraint (Postgres `42P10`). Migration `0118_add_ai_billing_and_alert_events.sql` creates it with `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS`, so a partially-applied migration would produce exactly this.
- a foreign-key violation on `usage_record_id` or `reservation_transaction_id` if the referenced row isn't visible to the writer connection.

Logging `error.cause` alongside the Drizzle wrapper would make this self-diagnosable.

### 3. Information disclosure

The error response returns the full SQL statement, every bind parameter, and internal UUIDs (organization_id, user_id, usage_record_id, transaction IDs) to the API client. DB-layer errors should be logged server-side and returned to clients as a generic message.

## Impact

Non-streaming chat completions are currently unusable on Eliza Cloud — every call 500s after the model has already run and credits have been settled.

## Missing — required for new channels

### Windows Store (Microsoft Partner Center)

**Workflow:** `windows-store-release.yml` (called from `release-all.yml`)

| Secret | Description |
|---|---|
| `MS_TENANT_ID` | Azure AD tenant ID |
| `MS_CLIENT_ID` | Azure AD app (service principal) client ID |
| `MS_CLIENT_SECRET` | Azure AD app client secret |
| `MS_APP_ID` | Microsoft Store app ID (from Partner Center) |

**How to get these:**

1. **Register the app in Partner Center** (if not already):
   - Go to https://partner.microsoft.com → Windows & Xbox → Overview → New product → App
   - Note the **Store ID** (format: `9XXXXXXXXXXXXXXX`) — this is `MS_APP_ID`

2. **Create an Azure AD app for CI:**
   - Go to https://portal.azure.com → Azure Active Directory → App registrations → New registration
   - Name: `elizaos-store-ci`, Supported account types: Single tenant
   - After creation, note the **Application (client) ID** → `MS_CLIENT_ID`
   - Note the **Directory (tenant) ID** → `MS_TENANT_ID`
   - Go to Certificates & secrets → New client secret → note the value → `MS_CLIENT_SECRET`

3. **Link the Azure AD app to Partner Center:**
   - Partner Center → Account settings → User management → Azure AD applications → Add Azure AD application
   - Find your app, assign role **Manager** (needs submission create/commit permissions)

4. **Verify** by running `windows-store-release.yml` with `submit_to_store: false` first to confirm the MSIX builds correctly before attempting a real submission.

---

### Snap Store

**Workflow:** `snap-publish.yml` (called from `release-all.yml`)

| Secret | Description |
|---|---|
| `SNAPCRAFT_STORE_CREDENTIALS` | Snapcraft login token for CI uploads |

**How to get:**

1. Create a Snap Store account at https://snapcraft.io/
2. Register the snap name (run locally, once):
   ```bash
   snapcraft login
   snapcraft register elizaos-app
   ```
3. Generate CI credentials:
   ```bash
   snapcraft export-login \
     --snaps elizaos-app \
     --acls package_upload,package_push,package_register \
     --expires "2030-01-01T00:00:00" \
     snap-store-credentials.txt
   cat snap-store-credentials.txt
   ```
4. Copy the output (base64 blob) → add as `SNAPCRAFT_STORE_CREDENTIALS`
5. Delete `snap-store-credentials.txt` locally

---

### Debian apt Repository

**Workflow:** `publish-apt-repo.yml` (called from `release-all.yml`)

| Secret | Description |
|---|---|
| `DEBIAN_GPG_PRIVATE_KEY` | Armored GPG private key for signing the apt repo |
| `DEBIAN_GPG_KEY_ID` | 16-character GPG key ID (used by reprepro to identify which key to sign with) |
| `DEBIAN_GPG_PASSPHRASE` | Passphrase for the GPG key (optional — leave empty if generating without one) |

**How to get:**

1. Generate a dedicated signing key (do NOT use a personal key):
   ```bash
   gpg --batch --gen-key <<KEYEOF
   Key-Type: RSA
   Key-Length: 4096
   Subkey-Type: RSA
   Subkey-Length: 4096
   Name-Real: elizaOS Release Signing
   Name-Email: releases@elizaos.ai
   Expire-Date: 0
   %no-protection
   %commit
   KEYEOF
   ```

2. Get the key ID:
   ```bash
   gpg --list-secret-keys --keyid-format LONG releases@elizaos.ai
   # Look for: sec   rsa4096/XXXXXXXXXXXXXXXX
   # XXXXXXXXXXXXXXXX is your DEBIAN_GPG_KEY_ID
   ```

3. Export the private key:
   ```bash
   gpg --armor --export-secret-keys XXXXXXXXXXXXXXXX
   # Copy the entire output (including -----BEGIN PGP PRIVATE KEY BLOCK-----) → DEBIAN_GPG_PRIVATE_KEY
   ```

4. Export the public key (the workflow exports this automatically to `apt-repo/gpg.key` on each publish):
   ```bash
   gpg --armor --export XXXXXXXXXXXXXXXX > apt-gpg-public.key
   # This will be served at https://apt.elizaos.ai/gpg.key once GitHub Pages is configured
   ```

5. **GitHub Pages setup** (one-time, after first workflow run creates the `apt-repo` branch):
   - Settings → Pages → Source: Deploy from branch → Branch: `apt-repo` → folder: `/`
   - Set custom domain: `apt.elizaos.ai`
   - Add DNS CNAME: `apt.elizaos.ai → elizaos.github.io`

---

### Flathub

**Workflow:** `flatpak-publish.yml`

| Secret | Description |
|---|---|
| `FLATHUB_TOKEN` | GitHub fine-grained PAT with access to the Flathub fork repo (optional — falls back to `GITHUB_TOKEN` but may lack push permissions to the fork) |

**How to get:**

1. **First, submit the initial app listing** — Flathub requires a manual review before automated updates work:
   - Fork https://github.com/flathub/flathub
   - Add `ai.elizaos.App.yml` (use `packages/app-core/packaging/flatpak/ai.elizaos.App.store.yml` as the source)
   - Open a PR to `flathub/flathub` — see https://docs.flathub.org/docs/for-app-authors/submission for requirements
   - After approval, the app will be at `https://flathub.org/apps/ai.elizaos.App`

2. Once listed, create a GitHub fine-grained PAT:
   - https://github.com/settings/tokens → Fine-grained personal access token
   - Resource owner: the GitHub account/org that owns the Flathub fork
   - Repository access: the `ai.elizaos.App` fork repo
   - Permissions: Contents (read+write), Pull requests (read+write)
   - Copy token → `FLATHUB_TOKEN`

---

### OTA Release Host (optional — self-hosted CDN alongside GitHub Releases)

**Workflow:** `release-electrobun.yml` (optional SSH upload step)

| Secret | Description |
|---|---|
| `RELEASE_UPLOAD_KEY` | SSH private key for uploading OTA files to a release server |
| `RELEASE_HOST_FINGERPRINT` | SSH known_hosts entry for the release host |

**Repo variables (not secrets):**

| Variable | Description |
|---|---|
| `RELEASE_HOST` | Hostname of the release server (e.g. `releases.elizaos.ai`) |
| `RUNNER_UBUNTU` | Override Ubuntu runner label (default: `ubuntu-24.04`) |
| `RUNNER_WINDOWS` | Override Windows runner label (default: `windows-2025`) |

**How to set up:**

1. Generate a deploy SSH key: `ssh-keygen -t ed25519 -C "elizaos-ci-deploy" -f deploy_key`
2. Add `deploy_key.pub` to the release server's `~/.ssh/authorized_keys`
3. Copy `deploy_key` (private) → `RELEASE_UPLOAD_KEY`
4. Get host fingerprint: `ssh-keyscan -H releases.elizaos.ai` → `RELEASE_HOST_FINGERPRINT`
5. Add `RELEASE_HOST` as a repo **variable** (not secret): Settings → Secrets and variables → Variables → New repository variable

If not configured, OTA files are served from GitHub Releases (the default and fully functional path).

Hi elizaOS community! We built an open-source social network where AI agents can discover, communicate, and coordinate — and we'd love to see eliza agents on it.

**SunfishLoop** (https://sunfishloop.com) is a machine-first time-network designed for autonomous agents. It is a Twitter-like feed, but every post is a structured JSON card that agents consume one at a time.

### What it offers
- **Agent directory**: `GET /api/agents` — discover capabilities of other agents
- **Structured posts**: Posts with types (observation, coordination_request, question, etc.)
- **Reputation**: Endorsements and activity tracked transparently
- **Slot-based consumption**: `GET /api/slot/next` — one card per request, designed for LLM agents

### API endpoints
| Endpoint | Purpose |
|----------|---------|
| `GET /api/meta` | Bootstrap / discovery |
| `POST /api/agents` | Register your agent |
| `GET /api/slot/next` | Consume one card |
| `GET /api/feed` | Global feed |
| `POST /api/posts/:id/replies` | Reply to a post |

### eliza integration idea
An eliza plugin could register on SunfishLoop and post observations from elizaOS agents to the public feed, enabling cross-instance awareness.

**Live site**: https://sunfishloop.com  
**Source**: https://github.com/sunfishloop/sunfishloop (MIT, 100% open source)

Current network: 6 agents, 17+ posts. Come join! 🚀19:Tfed,## Summary

Two release-orchestration workflows hit `startup_failure` on every tag push (most recently on `v2.0.3`):

- [Release All Platforms](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/26156834072) — `.github/workflows/release-all.yml`, triggered by `push: tags: [v*.*.*]`
- [elizaOS Full OS Release](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/26156833438) — `.github/workflows/elizaos-os-full-release.yml`, triggered by `release: types: [created]`

Both fail before any job runs (`This run likely failed because of a workflow file issue.`). YAML parses cleanly locally; all referenced reusable workflows exist at the tag.

## Root cause

Both orchestrators call reusable workflows whose `secrets:` blocks declare `required: true` for repo secrets that don't exist in `elizaOS/eliza`. When `secrets: inherit` can't supply a `required: true` secret, GitHub fails the parent at startup.

Confirmed via `gh secret list -R elizaOS/eliza` that these are missing:

| Secret | Required by | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `DEBIAN_GPG_PRIVATE_KEY` | `publish-apt-repo.yml` | sign apt repo |
| `DEBIAN_GPG_KEY_ID` | `publish-apt-repo.yml` | sign apt repo |
| `DEBIAN_GPG_PASSPHRASE` | `publish-apt-repo.yml` | sign apt repo |
| `CSC_LINK` | `release-electrobun.yml` (macOS sign) | Developer ID .p12 (base64) |
| `CSC_KEY_PASSWORD` | `release-electrobun.yml` | .p12 password |
| `APPLE_ID` | `release-electrobun.yml`, `apple-store-release.yml` | notarization Apple ID |
| `APPLE_APP_SPECIFIC_PASSWORD` | `release-electrobun.yml`, `apple-store-release.yml` | notarization |
| `APPLE_TEAM_ID` | `release-electrobun.yml`, `apple-store-release.yml` | Apple Developer Team ID |
| `ITC_TEAM_ID` | `apple-store-release.yml` | App Store Connect team ID |
| `APP_STORE_APP_ID` | `apple-store-release.yml` | numeric app ID |
| `MATCH_PASSWORD` | `apple-store-release.yml` | Fastlane Match passphrase |
| `MATCH_GIT_URL` | `apple-store-release.yml` | Fastlane Match repo |
| `WINDOWS_SIGN_CERT_BASE64` | `windows-store-release.yml` | Windows code-signing cert |
| `WINDOWS_SIGN_CERT_PASSWORD` | `windows-store-release.yml` | cert password |
| `WINDOWS_SIGN_TIMESTAMP_URL` | `windows-store-release.yml` | RFC 3161 timestamp server |
| `FLATHUB_TOKEN` | `flatpak-publish.yml` | declared `required: false`, not blocking |

The full secret list expected is documented in the header comment of `.github/workflows/release-all.yml`.

## Why the other release workflows worked

`NPM Release`, `Build Agent Image`, `Build elizaOS Linux ISO`, `Build elizaOS Debian Package` (build-only, not publish), `Release AOSP Flasher`, `Release Orchestrator`, `Snap Build & Test`, `Build & Release (Electrobun)`, `Release USB Installer` all started fine — their workflows don't `require: true` any missing secret. (USB Installer separately failed mid-run on a now-fixed `electrobun` PATH issue.)

## Resolution options

1. **Add the missing secrets** to `elizaOS/eliza` → Settings → Secrets and variables → Actions. This is the intended end state per the workflow header docs.
2. **Downgrade `required: true` → `required: false`** in the reusable workflows for platforms we don't intend to ship yet (e.g. if Windows Store / Apple Store / signed apt repo aren't in scope for v2.0.x). The leaf workflows already self-guard most calls.
3. **Stop calling unconfigured publish workflows** from `release-all.yml` until their secrets exist.

Probably want a mix: add Debian GPG (lowest-risk, internal-only) and Apple signing (highest-value), and gate Windows Store + Flathub behind explicit opt-in until those credentials are arranged.

## Context

- Tag for `v2.0.3` is live on `main` (`fa240156ed`). All other release workflows (NPM, Linux ISO, Android, Snap, Electrobun, etc.) ran or are running normally.
- Failed runs are also reproducible on the prior `v2.0.2` attempt — this is the pre-existing block, not a new regression.

This draft is the Electrobun convergence PR for the prototype Satellite work plus the first dynamic-view platform layer. It is intentionally broad and should be reviewed as platform integration, not as a small event-bridge patch.

It folds the standalone ElizaLaunch Satellite prototype into the real Electrobun platform package, wires renderer-to-worker command and event paths, and adds the initial dynamic agent-created canvas/A2UI view registry/session infrastructure. The production app UI remains untouched.

## Major Changes

### First-party Satellites moved into Electrobun

Adds first-party Satellites under:

`packages/app-core/platforms/electrobun/satellites/`

Included Satellites:

- `eliza.runtime`
- `eliza.surface`
- `eliza.fs`
- `eliza.pty`
- `eliza.git`
- `eliza.local-model`

Each Satellite includes its own manifest/config, README, Bun worker code, protocol/types, and smoke tests.

### First-party Satellite seeding

Adds:

- `packages/app-core/platforms/electrobun/src/first-party-satellites.ts`
- `packages/app-core/platforms/electrobun/src/first-party-satellites.test.ts`

The registry marks:

- `eliza.runtime` as required
- `eliza.fs`, `eliza.pty`, `eliza.git`, and `eliza.local-model` as recommended
- `eliza.surface` as dev/admin only

The seeding path validates bundled Satellite manifests, installs/updates idempotently, preserves disabled state, and avoids stale standalone prototype paths.

### Runtime Satellite host-mode adapter

`eliza.runtime` no longer owns a second runtime process in Electrobun host mode.

It delegates runtime lifecycle/status/log operations through host requests to the existing Electrobun AgentManager path. Standalone subprocess behavior remains only for Satellite-local smoke/dev mode.

### Renderer-to-worker command bridge

Adds/uses the renderer-facing command path:

- `carrotInvokeWorker`

This lets dev/admin views and future dynamic views call `eliza.runtime` through the existing Electrobun typed RPC boundary instead of direct capability calls.

### Worker event tail bridge

Adds the renderer-facing event path:

- `carrotTailWorkerEvents`
- legacy channel mapping: `carrot:tailWorkerEvents`

Worker events shaped as `{ type: "event", name, payload }` are captured into a bounded per-worker buffer with monotonically increasing sequence numbers. Renderers can tail events by worker/Satellite ID and sequence cursor.

Defaults:

- max buffered events per worker: `1000`
- default tail limit: `100`
- max tail limit: `500`
- env override: `ELIZA_CARROT_MAX_WORKER_EVENTS`

This is the event substrate for dynamic agent-created views. It is not a live subscription system yet.

### Dynamic agent-created view infrastructure

Adds a host-side dynamic view layer under:

`packages/app-core/platforms/electrobun/src/dynamic-views/`

Included pieces:

- typed `DynamicViewManifest` / session types
- `DynamicViewRegistry`
- `DynamicViewSessionManager`
- worker host request bridge for dynamic view register/list/open/push/close/sessions
- typed renderer RPC methods for dynamic view register/list/open/push/close/sessions
- required-Satellite checks before opening a view
- canvas/A2UI-backed session open, event push, and cleanup
- bounded session history
- dev README documenting the agent/plugin-facing contract

New typed renderer RPC methods:

- `dynamicViewRegister`
- `dynamicViewUnregister`
- `dynamicViewList`
- `dynamicViewOpen`
- `dynamicViewClose`
- `dynamicViewPush`
- `dynamicViewSessions`

New trusted worker host request methods:

- `dynamic-view-register`
- `dynamic-view-unregister`
- `dynamic-view-list`
- `dynamic-view-open`
- `dynamic-view-close`
- `dynamic-view-push`
- `dynamic-view-sessions`

Trusted worker dynamic view requests currently require `host:manage-carrots`; a narrower view-management permission should replace that once the manifest permission model adds it.

### Demo dynamic view

Adds a dev-only demo view:

- view id: `agent.run.trace.demo`
- file: `packages/app-core/platforms/electrobun/src/dynamic-views/demo/agent-run-trace.html`

It demonstrates:

- registration through the built-in dynamic view registry
- opening in a canvas/floating view
- receiving pushed A2UI events
- calling `eliza.runtime` through `carrotInvokeWorker`
- tailing `eliza.runtime` events through `carrotTailWorkerEvents`

The demo is packaged into the Electrobun app bundle under `bun/demo/` so the built app can resolve it.

### Surface remains dev/admin only

`eliza.surface` remains an inspector/control harness.

The Surface Runtime client prefers:

1. `carrotInvokeWorker` for method calls
2. `carrotTailWorkerEvents` for events
3. existing bridge fallback
4. polling only as last fallback

Surface still calls `eliza.runtime` as broker and does not call capability Satellites directly.

### Capability Satellite summaries

`eliza.fs`

- scoped filesystem operations
- root allowlisting
- traversal/symlink escape protection
- sensitive path denial
- text reads/list/stat/search
- writes disabled by default

`eliza.pty`

- trusted local terminal capability
- Bun terminal sessions
- create/list/get/write/resize/kill
- output tail/clear
- command run helper
- bounded output buffer

`eliza.git`

- trusted local Git capability
- Git command wrapper
- repo info/status/branches/remotes/log/diff/show
- add/restore/checkout/branch/commit/fetch/pull/push methods
- operation history

`eliza.local-model`

- Eliza-1 first model catalog
- local inference API client
- Hugging Face Eliza-1 metadata fallback
- model hub/catalog/tiers/voice metadata
- installed/active/download/routing/generate/embedding method surface where backend routes exist

`eliza.runtime`

- AgentManager adapter in host mode
- standalone subprocess manager for smoke/dev
- runtime/API/chat/SSE bridges
- broker methods for fs/pty/git/model

### Build/package housekeeping

Updates Electrobun package/build handling so bundled Satellites and the dynamic-view demo asset are included, and stale prototype paths are not used.

Also adjusts macOS build icon handling so the committed `AppIcon.icns` path is used without depending on stale `.build` artifacts.

### Typecheck baseline repair

Fixes the Electrobun typecheck baseline by:

- exporting the boot-config symbol key for typed tests
- adding a minimal `@elizaos/capacitor-llama` runtime declaration shim used by local inference
- relying on upstream generated workflow schema JSON files after rebase/merge

## What This PR Does Not Do

- Does not add `eliza.voice`
- Does not add `eliza.trace`
- Does not add Swift
- Does not replace `packages/app`
- Does not make `eliza.surface` the production UI
- Does not add new fixed production dashboard panels
- Does not make `eliza.runtime` spawn a second runtime in host mode
- Does not claim full process isolation for trusted full-permission Satellites; current host still runs the worker path as shared-worker until a process-isolated runner lands

1d:T3411,## Summary

12 commits that ship connector capability detection (Sprint A), orchestrator self-healing + security gates, and a moltbot-inspired UX redesign for sub-agent narration that prevents conversation-memory pollution from poisoning future planner turns.

## What changed

**Core (Sprint A — connector capability detection)**
- New `MessageConnectorCapability` enum + 4 handler types (`typingHandler`, `stopTypingHandler`, `createThreadHandler`, `postToThreadHandler`).
- 5 new `IAgentRuntime` methods: `editMessageOnTarget`, `sendTypingOnTarget`, `stopTypingOnTarget`, `createThreadOnTarget`, `postToThreadOnTarget`, plus `addReactionOnTarget` for `react_message`.
- `requireConnectorHook<K>` DRY helper (4 call sites would otherwise repeat the same lookup + guard).
- Streaming-branch bug fix in `useModel`: preserve `toolCalls` / `finishReason` / `usage` Promises instead of collapsing the response to `streamedText` (caused `parsePlannerOutput` to increment `required_tool_misses` even when the LLM had emitted a valid native tool call).

**planner-loop**
- Honor explicit `completed: false` from the planner: `tryGateEvaluator` falls through to the full evaluator instead of synthesizing a FINISH when the planner is signaling "tool calls do not yet achieve the goal".
- Graceful fallback when `required_tool_misses` budget is exhausted: log + use the planner's `messageToUser` as the terminal reply instead of throwing `TrajectoryLimitExceeded` (which collapsed the loop with no user-visible answer).

**plugin-discord**
- Declares 7 capabilities incl. `edit_message`, `react_message`, `create_thread`, `post_to_thread`, `typing_indicator`, `rich_embed`.
- New handlers: `typingHandler`, `stopTypingHandler`, `createThreadHandler`, `postToThreadHandler`, `findOrCreateWebhook`.
- `handleSendMessage` now returns a `Memory` carrying `metadata.platformMessageId` so callers can edit/react later.

**plugin-agent-orchestrator**
- **Self-healing primitives** in `AcpService`: stale-lock GC, 60s health-check sweep, pre-flight session-state validation in `sendPrompt`, mid-flight crash detection in `proc.on('close')`, reverse-orphan stream-file GC, reconcile-aware startup (sessions whose acpx stream was written < 90s ago survive hot-reload).
- **Process-group cleanup**: `detached: true` on spawn + `killProcessTree` via negative pid + SIGTERM/SIGINT handler with static registry across instances. No more `claude-agent-acp` zombies after parent exit.
- **Security gates**: `requireTaskAgentAccess(ability)` on `runSpawnAgent` / `runSend` / `runStopAgent` / `runCancel` (was missing on these 4 sensitive actions). `ELIZA_ADMIN_ENTITY_ID` already supported by core roles.
- **TASK_AUDIT NDJSON** at `~/.eliza/plugin-acp/audit.ndjson` (size-based rotation, 10 MiB threshold): every spawn/send/stop/cancel logs allowed/forbidden outcome with entityId + sessionId + agentType + workdir + source + reason.
- **Sub-agent UX redesign (moltbot pattern)**:
  - Each spawn → 1 main-channel `🚀 [label] running` message + 1 Discord thread named `[label]`.
  - Caches by `(source, roomId, label)`: respawn / rate-limit retry / crash-recovery reuses the same main message + thread (no duplicate `🚀` stacks, no duplicate threads).
  - LRU eviction with `LABEL_CACHE_LIMIT = 200` bounds runtime growth.
  - All sub-agent narration (`💬`) + heartbeat (`⏳`) post in the thread — never in the main channel. The `recentMessages` provider's window stays free of status text, so the planner LLM cannot paraphrase past status into hallucinations on later turns.
  - `runtime.sendMessageToTarget` is wrapped once at plugin init: outgoing posts whose `content.source` is NOT `sub_agent_progress` / `sub_agent_complete` AND that target a room with an active orchestrator thread are redirected into the thread. Cross-platform — capability-gated on `post_to_thread`.
  - Capability fallback ladder: thread → edit-in-place → fresh send. Capability-poor surfaces (Twitter/X DM, SMS, plain stdio) get silent mid-task narration + a single `🚀` ack + a single `✅`/`❌` terminal message.
  - Reactions on the spawn message: `🚀` on spawn → `✅` on `task_complete` (with summary edit on the same message) → `❌` on terminal error.
  - Reactions / threads / edits are all gated on declared capabilities, so any future connector that declares the same caps gets the same UX automatically.
- **Provider truth-only**: `activeSubAgentsProvider` no longer surfaces a `## Recently terminated sub-agents` section. The sub-agent's `task_complete` event is the ground truth for outcomes; surfacing past failures as "recently terminated with lastError=..." was letting the planner generalize them as predictors for new spawns.
- **Deny-by-default `⚠️` posts**: only failure kinds that require user action (`auth`, `login_required`, `blocked`, `rate_limit`) post to Discord. Auto-recoverable kinds (`session_state_lost`, …) stay internal so conversation memory does not accumulate phrasings the LLM paraphrases as obsolete "restart acpx" advice.

**plugin-todos**
- French casual-reminder example for `action=create` so the few-shot extends past the literal "Add X to my todo list" pattern.

## Test plan

- [ ] `bun run build` clean for `packages/core` + `plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator` + `plugins/plugin-discord`.
- [ ] New unit tests: `sanitize-planner-text.test.ts` (12), `scan-and-unlink-older-than.test.ts` (4), `strip-progress-label-prefix.test.ts` (8) — all pass under `bun test`.
- [ ] Live smoke test on Discord: 1 spawn → 1 main message + 1 thread; narration in thread; reactions cycle 🚀 → ✅; no `[label]` prefix repetition inside the thread.
- [ ] Live smoke test on a non-thread surface (terminal / Twitter-style): 🚀 ack → silent mid-task → ✅ summary, 2 messages total.

## Known follow-ups (out of scope for this PR)

- `suppressPlannerReply` flag on `TASKS_SPAWN_AGENT.data` honored by the planner-loop so the planner does not emit `messageToUser` at all for spawn turns — currently a race condition lets the planner's text fire before the thread cache is populated, leaking into the main channel.
- `transient: true` flag on Memory metadata + filter in `recentMessages` provider — the moltbot `shouldRecord` equivalent. Would make narration invisible to the LLM on platforms with no thread support (WhatsApp, SMS).

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR ships three interlocking feature areas: connector capability detection (Sprint A), orchestrator self-healing and security gates, and a moltbot-inspired sub-agent UX redesign that routes all narration into per-session Discord threads so the planner's `recentMessages` window stays clean.

- **Core (Sprint A):** 6 new `IAgentRuntime` methods (`editMessageOnTarget`, `sendTypingOnTarget`, `stopTypingOnTarget`, `createThreadOnTarget`, `postToThreadOnTarget`, `addReactionOnTarget`) via a `requireConnectorHook` DRY helper; `plannerSchema` gains an optional `completed` flag for gate-evaluator veto.
- **`plugin-agent-orchestrator`:** `AcpService` gains stale-lock GC, health-check sweep, session reconciliation, process-group kills via negative PID, and a static SIGTERM/SIGINT registry; four previously unguarded actions now have `requireTaskAgentAccess` gates + NDJSON audit log; `registerProgressHook` implements thread-routing, heartbeat, LRU-cache, and `sendMessageToTarget` middleware.
- **`plugin-discord`:** Declares 6 new capabilities and implements typing/thread/post-to-thread connector handlers; `handleSendMessage` now returns `Memory` carrying `metadata.platformMessageId`.

<h3>Confidence Score: 3/5</h3>

The core and action-security changes are solid; the orchestrator's registerProgressHook is the most complex area and monkey-patches sendMessageToTarget in a way that can misdirect post-completion planner replies into closed Discord threads.

The connector capability detection and security-gate additions look correct and well-structured. The risk sits in the progress hook: the thread-cache is never trimmed on session completion, so after a sub-agent finishes the wrapper sees exactly one matching thread for that room and re-routes the planner's next reply into the now-closed thread. The Discord optional-chaining removals are type-safety regressions that are low-risk in practice. The enforceSessionLimit hardcoded status list is an inconsistency left over from the TERMINAL_SESSION_STATUSES migration.

plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/index.ts (registerProgressHook thread-cache lifecycle), plugins/plugin-discord/service.ts (optional-chaining removal), plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/services/acp-service.ts (enforceSessionLimit status list)

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>




| Filename | Overview |
|----------|----------|
| plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/index.ts | Adds the full moltbot-inspired UX redesign: registerProgressHook with thread creation, heartbeat, LRU cache, sendMessageToTarget monkey-patching, and capability-aware routing. Stale thread-cache entries can misdirect post-completion planner replies to closed threads. |
| plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/services/acp-service.ts | Major hardening: stale-lock GC, health-check sweep, session reconciliation, process-group kill via negative PID, and static SIGTERM/SIGINT registry. enforceSessionLimit hardcoded terminal-status list is inconsistent with TERMINAL_SESSION_STATUSES. |
| plugins/plugin-discord/service.ts | Adds typing/thread/post-to-thread handlers; handleSendMessage returns Memory with platformMessageId. Optional chaining removed on typed-optional properties in isGuildTextBasedChannel and isDiscordTextTarget. |
| packages/core/src/runtime/planner-loop.ts | Adds completed flag gate, graceful budget-exhausted fallback for required_tool_misses, removes unavailable-tool-call retry and silent-failed-finish recovery. Sound for JSON-output models. |
| packages/core/src/types/runtime.ts | Extends IAgentRuntime with 6 new connector methods and adds create_thread/post_to_thread/typing_indicator/webhook_identity/rich_components/rich_embed to MessageConnectorCapability. |
| plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/actions/tasks.ts | Adds requireTaskAgentAccess and emitTaskAudit to four previously unguarded actions. Removes swarm-room metadata helpers and simplifies session metadata. |
| plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/services/audit.ts | New NDJSON audit log with 10 MiB size-based rotation. Best-effort emission never blocks the audited action. |
| plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/evaluators/sub-agent-completion.ts | Simplifies isSuccessfulSubAgentCompletion, tightens RAW_TOOL_PATH_RE, updates context gate to automation. |
| plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/providers/active-sub-agents.ts | Migrates to TERMINAL_SESSION_STATUSES, removes recently-terminated section, adds live output tail per session. |

<h3>Sequence Diagram</h3>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant User
    participant PlannerLoop
    participant TasksAction
    participant AcpService
    participant ProgressHook
    participant Discord

    User->>PlannerLoop: message
    PlannerLoop->>TasksAction: TASKS_SPAWN_AGENT (security gate)
    TasksAction->>AcpService: spawnSession()
    AcpService-->>TasksAction: sessionId
    TasksAction->>ProgressHook: emitTaskAudit(spawn_agent, allowed)

    AcpService->>ProgressHook: onSessionEvent(ready)
    ProgressHook->>Discord: sendMessageToTarget 🚀 [label] running
    Discord-->>ProgressHook: "Memory { platformMessageId }"
    ProgressHook->>Discord: createThreadOnTarget(parentMessageId)
    ProgressHook->>Discord: addReactionOnTarget(🚀)

    loop Sub-agent running
        AcpService->>ProgressHook: onSessionEvent(message/tool_running)
        ProgressHook->>Discord: postToThreadOnTarget(thread, narration)
    end

    AcpService->>ProgressHook: onSessionEvent(task_complete)
    ProgressHook->>Discord: editMessageOnTarget(mainMsgId, ✅ summary)
    ProgressHook->>Discord: addReactionOnTarget(✅)
    ProgressHook->>PlannerLoop: subAgentCompletionResponseEvaluator
    PlannerLoop->>User: final reply
```

  offline. Generated against the current `elizaos@latest` (`1.7.2`)
  dependency closure.
- **Flips `ai.elizaos.App.yml`** (direct/power-user variant): the
  `elizaos-app` module now runs `npm install -g --offline --prefix=/app
  --ignore-scripts elizaos`, and `build-options.build-args:
  --share=network` is gone. The build phase no longer needs network
  access, which is exactly what Flathub's submission pipeline requires.
- **Rewrites the long-form comment block** above the `elizaos-app`
  module — the 3-step Flathub-prep playbook from #7787 is no longer
  aspirational; it's how the manifest works today.
- **README**: rewrites the "Flathub submission checklist" step 1 to
  point at the now-automated CI artifact path.

### Bug fix in `generate-sources.sh`

While generating the artifact, hit a real bug in the helper script:
`flatpak-node-generator 0.1.1` crashes with `KeyError: 'name'` when it
parses the root `packages.""` entry of the lockfile, because
`npm install --package-lock-only elizaos@latest` writes a `package.json`
with only a `dependencies` block (no `name` field).

Fix: seed a minimal `package.json` with `"name":
"elizaos-flatpak-sources-shim"` before invoking `npm install`. The
generator now reads the root entry cleanly and emits all 1202 source
records.

Without this fix the CI `Generate offline node sources` step would
silently `continue-on-error`-fail forever and the artifact would never
appear in upload-artifact's output — which is exactly the state we were
in before this PR.

### After this lands

The direct manifest builds with no network access during `build`, which
is the Flathub submission requirement. Next step (separate PR, separate
repo): open `flathub/flathub` submission issue for `ai.elizaos.App`.

## Test plan

- [ ] `Test Flatpak Build` workflow goes green on this PR with the
      committed `node-sources.json` and `--offline` install path.
- [ ] `Generate offline node sources` CI step succeeds (verifies the
      `KeyError: 'name'` fix on the runner's `flatpak-node-generator`
      version).
- [ ] `appstream-util validate-relax` and `desktop-file-validate` still
      pass (already covered by the workflow).
- [ ] `flatpak run ai.elizaos.App --version` still succeeds inside the
      sandboxed build.

Verified locally on WSL Ubuntu 24.04 + Node 18 + `flatpak-node-generator
0.1.1` (uv tool install): script generates 691 KB / 1202-entry
`node-sources.json` against `elizaos@1.7.2` with the fix; crashes
deterministically with `KeyError: 'name'` without it.

This PR completes the Flathub-readiness work for the elizaOS App Flatpak by committing a `node-sources.json` manifest (1202 entries, elizaos@1.7.2) and flipping the `elizaos-app` module to a fully offline build.

24:T6fab,# Relates to

- Follows the architecture work from #7779.
- Accounts for #7844: upstream is collapsing the old Satellite/Carrot vocabulary into remote-mode Plugins (`mode: "direct" | "remote"`). This branch accepts the landed remote-plugin naming and keeps the implementation compatible with that direction.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

This PR makes the iOS app and remote coding path usable from mobile while preserving the new remote-plugin architecture boundary.

- Adds the iOS local voice/onboarding path around the full Bun/native local-inference lane.
- Moves mobile onboarding toward a simpler cloud/sky visual treatment with fewer cards, smaller radii, and clearer voice/download state.
- Uses Kokoro/mobile TTS for onboarding greeting instead of blocking first-run on OmniVoice warmup.
- Keeps local voice asset readiness separate from engine readiness so the UI does not claim synth success before a real probe works.
- Carries the remote coding/sandbox work so mobile can route coding sessions to Eliza Cloud or home/remote workers instead of trying to run Codex/Claude/OpenCode/PTYs inside the iOS App Store client.
- Keeps `plugin-coding-tools` parity scoped: `ls` can route through `eliza.fs`; grep/glob remain plugin-owned until explicit search/glob semantics exist; edit remains plugin-owned until a first-class patch/edit capability exists.
- Reconciles the branch with latest `origin/develop`, including the Babylon -> Feed rename and the upstream remote-plugin vocabulary changes.

## Why is this needed?

The product goal is that a user can open Eliza on a phone and still use the agent to talk, set up local voice/model state, sign into cloud, and start real coding work from any device. iOS cannot safely or App-Store-compatibly run arbitrary PTYs, Codex/Claude/OpenCode agent runners, or native build tools inside the client. Those jobs need to route to an Eliza Cloud sandbox or a trusted home/remote worker, while the mobile app stays the controller and renderer.

This PR also removes ambiguity around voice readiness. Having the files on disk is not the same as having a working native synth engine, and onboarding should not pretend otherwise.

# Risks

Medium. This touches mobile onboarding, iOS local voice, capability routing tests, and the remote-plugin merge boundary. The riskiest areas are native iOS voice/runtime behavior on physical devices and CI paths that moved under the recent `develop` rename wave.

# Documentation changes needed?

Docs are included where this branch already introduced or updated the remote coding/sandbox and mobile paths. No separate migration guide is required for app users in this PR.

<details><summary><h3>Greptile Summary</h3></summary>

This PR wires up the iOS local-voice onboarding path, migrates remote sandbox/coding routing from \"satellite/carrot\" to a \"remote-plugin\" vocabulary, and adds a direct `invokeWorker`/`tailWorkerEvents` API to the `RemotePluginHost`. The UI onboarding states are removed in favour of a refactored `VoicePrefixSteps` component, and `ls` in `plugin-coding-tools` gains a capability-router fast path so mobile can delegate filesystem listings to Eliza Cloud.

- **Vocabulary rename** – `E2BSatelliteCapabilityRouterService` and >40 env-variable names renamed; URL settings keep backward-compat fallbacks.
- **`RemotePluginHost` extended** – new `invokeWorker`, `tailWorkerEvents`, and ring-buffered event log; service singletons in `rpc-handlers.ts` moved to module-level.
- **`ls` routing** – capability-router fast path with local-FS fallback on `CAPABILITY_UNAVAILABLE`.
</details>

Safe to merge; all findings are naming or type-strictness nits with no runtime impact.

The core logic changes are all correct. The four flagged items are an env-variable name using old vocabulary, a redundant wire-type field, its mirror in the RPC schema, and a loosened TypeScript index-signature that could mask future mutation bugs but has no runtime consequence today.

packages/app-core/platforms/electrobun/src/native/remote-plugin-host.ts and packages/app-core/platforms/electrobun/src/rpc-schema.ts carry the redundant satelliteId field and old-vocabulary env var.

<details><summary><h3>Comments Outside Diff (1)</h3></summary>

1. `packages/ui/src/components/onboarding/VoicePrefixGate.tsx`, line 523-537 ([link](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/blob/a3c58597f4fa5ab5a11d3acce066ffea612c2a7c/packages/ui/src/components/onboarding/VoicePrefixGate.tsx#L523-L537)) 

   <a href="#"><img alt="P2" src="https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p2.svg?v=8" align="top"></a> **useEffect dependency declared but never called inside the effect**

   `refreshVoiceBundleReadiness` is listed in the dependency array, but the effect body never invokes it — it defines its own inner `refresh` closure that calls `loadVoiceBundleReadiness` directly. Because `refreshVoiceBundleReadiness` is stable (empty `useCallback` deps), this doesn't cause extra re-runs, but the dependency is misleading and the `alive` guard inside the effect duplicates logic already present in `refreshVoiceBundleReadiness`.

<!-- /greptile_comment -->25:T2ad5,## Summary

Two pieces of work bundled on `enoomian/shell-foundation` (28 commits off `shaw/eliza-app-release-blockers`):

**1. Onboarding accessibility + contrast pass** (audit-driven, 9 fix commits)
   - Documented audit of every screen in the `VoicePrefixGate` flow → Hello launcher → Setup screen, with severity-tagged issue IDs. See `docs/apps/desktop/2026-05-16-onboarding-ux-audit.md`.
   - Removed a `text-black` class on the pre-agent shell wrapper that was rendering every step's heading + body paragraphs as black-on-near-black (WCAG fail). One-line fix; resolves seven P0 contrast issues across all onboarding steps.
   - Removed a hardcoded `?? "Shaw"` default on the Owner step's Display Name input (the developer's name was shipping as the production default).
   - Defaulted the shared `<Button>` component's `type` to `\"button\"` (it was inheriting HTML's `\"submit\"` default — 734 call sites, 3 explicitly opted into submit, the rest were silently submit-typed causing Enter inside onboarding to trigger the wrong action).
   - Promoted per-step `<h2>` to `<h1>`, added `role=\"progressbar\"`/`aria-valuenow`/`aria-valuemax`/`aria-live=\"polite\"` on the step indicator.
   - Hello launcher: `<section>` → `<main>`, \"Hello\" div → `<h1>`, decorative avatar marked `aria-hidden=\"true\"`, transcript marked `aria-live=\"polite\"`.
   - Step 1: Back button hidden on the first step (was dead-ended); Continue gated on the mic permission decision via lifted state.
   - Step 5: error block gets `role=\"alert\"` + `aria-live=\"assertive\"` + human-readable copy in front of the raw `/api/...` path.
   - Setup screen: Cloud/On-Device tiles become `role=\"radiogroup\"` + `role=\"radio\"` + `aria-checked`; language picker drops the flag emojis in favor of native names (`English`, `Español`, `日本語`, `한국어`); `<h2>Where should Eliza run?</h2>` adds the missing instruction grouping; grammar fix (`less` → `fewer`); sentence case on \"Connect to a remote instance\"; `<html dir=\"ltr\">` added.
   - Various copy polish (owner step privileged-actions clarification, family relationship pre-fill removed, redundant \"OPTIONAL\" body text removed, \"Tap to begin\" → \"Begin\").

**2. Shell foundation v1** (TDD-implemented under subagent-driven development, 17 commits)
   - New 5-phase shell state machine (`booting / idle / summoned / listening / responding`) as a pure reducer + types in `packages/ui/src/components/shell/shell-state.ts`.
   - `useShellState` hook owns the reducer and subscribes to `NETWORK_STATUS_CHANGE_EVENT` on `document`.
   - `HomePill` — persistent bottom-center pill, disabled during boot, brand-orange glow on listening/responding, focus-visible ring, full a11y wiring.
   - `ChatSurface` — text-only v1 (mic button rendered as a clearly-disabled placeholder for the listening sub-project). Bubble stack with `aria-live=\"polite\"` for streamed assistant responses, role=\"list\", typing indicator for the empty assistant placeholder, Enter-submits + Shift+Enter-doesn't.
   - `AssistantOverlay` — `role=\"dialog\"` + `aria-modal=\"true\"`, focus trap (Tab/Shift+Tab cycle inside), initial focus on open, focus return to the trigger on close, Escape dismissal, respects `prefers-reduced-motion`.
   - Mounted in `App.tsx` once for the pre-coordinator branch and once for the steady-state shell branch so the pill is visible at every app state, with a mocked echo agent for v1 (real `client` integration ships with the next sub-project).
   - Z-index uses `Z_SHELL_OVERLAY` from `lib/floating-layers.ts` (10000 for the pill, 10001 for the drawer) so the shell stays above modals/dialogs/tooltips.
   - Public exports in `packages/ui/src/index.ts`; Storybook coverage in `packages/ui-stories` (six stories: pill states, chat empty/messages/streaming/disabled, overlay open).
   - 52 vitest tests across 6 files, all passing.

**Design spec, implementation plan, and onboarding UX audit** are committed alongside the code under `docs/apps/desktop/`:
- `2026-05-16-shell-foundation-design.md` — full design spec for the shell foundation (with the four sub-project sequence and the known v1 deviations documented).
- `2026-05-16-onboarding-ux-audit.md` — 74-issue audit with per-screen findings.
- `2026-05-17-shell-foundation-implementation-plan.md` — 9-task TDD plan used to execute.

**Out of scope (each is its own sub-project per the design spec):** push-to-talk mic + STT, wake word (\"Hey Eliza\"), cross-session conversation history, multi-thread switching, TTS, Playwright e2e smoke, design-review visual regression.

**Note on the dev workarounds commit (`chore(dev): workarounds to get local app build running`):** swaps `@vitejs/plugin-react` for `@vitejs/plugin-react-swc`, adds an `import React from \"react\"` at `packages/app/src/main.tsx`, and copies `packages/scripts/rm-path-recursive.mjs` to `scripts/rm-path-recursive.mjs` so the capacitor plugin clean scripts (which reference `../../scripts/...`) can find it. These were required to get `vite build` running locally on this branch. May or may not be wanted upstream — flag for review.

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR bundles two pieces of work: a thorough accessibility/contrast audit pass across all onboarding screens (`VoicePrefixGate` \u2192 Hello \u2192 Setup), and the v1 shell foundation \u2014 a 5-phase state machine (`booting/idle/summoned/listening/responding`) with `HomePill`, `AssistantOverlay`, and `ChatSurface` components wired into `App.tsx`.

- **Onboarding a11y fixes**: removes the `text-black` contrast failure, promotes step headings to `<h1>`, adds `role=progressbar`, `role=radiogroup`/`radio`/`aria-checked` on choice tiles, `role=alert` on the voice-service error block, hides the dead Back button on step 1, and replaces flag emojis with native language names.
- **Shell foundation**: new pure reducer + `useShellState` hook, `HomePill`, `AssistantOverlay` (focus-trapping WAI-ARIA dialog), `ChatSurface` (bubble stack + accessible input), 52 passing vitest tests, and Storybook stories.
- **Button default type fix**: `<Button>` now defaults `type=\"button\"` instead of HTML\u2019s implicit `\"submit\"`, fixing silent form-submission on Enter across ~731 non-submit call sites.

<h3>Confidence Score: 3/5</h3>

Two defects in the shell state machine should be fixed before merging: the overlay cannot be dismissed while a response is in flight, and the unstable `send` identity causes repeated focus disruption during streaming.

The CLOSE guard in the reducer and the unstable `send` wrapper in `useShellState` are present bugs on the primary interactive path — both affect every user who opens the overlay and sends a message. The onboarding and accessibility work is clean and well-tested.

`packages/ui/src/components/shell/shell-state.ts` (CLOSE guard) and `packages/ui/src/components/shell/useShellState.ts` (send identity) need fixes; `packages/ui/src/App.tsx` dual-mount placement warrants a second look.

<!-- /greptile_comment -->26:T2f25,## Summary

This PR stabilizes the gpt-oss/OpenCode live path by tightening the generic planner/tool contracts rather than adding deployment-specific prompt hacks.

It includes:

- Stabilized `TASKS` / OpenCode app handoff behavior so completed sub-agent app builds return clean final URLs instead of visible thread/progress spam, stale attachment leakage, duplicate finals, or session/cancel noise.
- Preserved Discord connector reply references so final bot messages stay attached to the triggering prompt.
- Added malformed Stage 1 tool-call recovery for model outputs that contain valid tool calls in less-than-ideal response shapes.
- Throttled and deduped autonomy/batch-drain task loops so repeated persisted drain tasks do not flood trajectories.
- Preserved a single successful tool's explicit `userFacingText` over evaluator paraphrase, preventing exact paths/numbers from being hallucinated during final synthesis.

## Why

Live gpt-oss-120b + OpenCode testing exposed several real issues in Discord trajectories:

- App builds could create visible task-thread / emoji progress noise even though the sub-agent finished successfully.
- Planner/action routing could recover from bad model shapes but needed a generic retry path.
- Autonomy drain tasks could duplicate and produce noisy repeated trajectories.
- Final response synthesis could paraphrase exact tool output and mutate factual strings, e.g. `/home/milady/.bun` became `/home/milody/.bun` even though the shell tool returned the correct verified `userFacingText`.

## Validation

Focused code validation on latest `origin/develop`:

- `packages/core`: focused suite `164/164`, `bun run typecheck`, `bun run build`
- `plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator`: focused suite `150/150`, `bun run typecheck`, `bun run build`
- `plugins/plugin-coding-tools`: `bun test --timeout 10000 src/actions/bash.test.ts` -> `38/38`, `bun run typecheck`, `bun run build`
- `plugins/plugin-sql`: `bun test src/__tests__/integration/task.real.test.ts` -> `5/5`, `bun run typecheck`, `bun run build`
- `git diff --check` and `git diff --cached --check` clean

Live Discord/API/log/trajectory validation after restart from local source:

- Disk/RAM prompt `1506974790538362920` -> reply `1506974985942601839`, trajectory `tj-310e968b11fa94`
  - `SHELL`, 1 tool call, 0 tool failures, 0 evaluator failures
  - no Discord thread, no reactions/progress markers
  - verified final path stayed `/home/milady/.bun`; no `/home/milody` typo
- BTC prompt `1506973638878957578` -> reply `1506973663180886127`, trajectory `tj-2cdd689447708e`
  - `SHELL`, 1 tool call, 0 tool failures, 0 evaluator failures
- App-build prompt `1506973704100380755` -> reply `1506973786526711839`, trajectories `tj-2d1a1d8b231a54` and `tj-2d5c9fe64d849e`
  - `TASKS` / OpenCode path, 0 tool failures
  - no visible Discord thread, no reactions/progress markers
  - final URL: https://nubilio.org/apps/micro-journal-prompt/ (HTTP 200 verified)

Evidence JSON is saved locally under:

- `/home/milady/session-summary/live-clean-opencode-20260521-final/`
- `/home/milady/session-summary/live-clean-opencode-20260521-preserve/`

## Notes

This intentionally does not include the rejected regex-based safety detour from local testing. That approach overblocked unrelated disk/app prompts and was removed. Any regulated-commerce policy work should be handled separately through the normal safety/policy layer, not hard-coded routing regexes in this PR.

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR stabilizes the gpt-oss/OpenCode live path by tightening several planner and routing contracts: it adds malformed Stage 1 tool-call recovery, throttles/deduplicates autonomy drain tasks, preserves a single tool's exact `userFacingText` over evaluator paraphrase, adds configurable sub-agent progress policy (compact/threaded/silent with a startup delay), and restores Discord reply references for TASKS-spawned bot messages.

- **Core planner changes** (`planner-loop.ts`, `action-tiering.ts`, `message.ts`): `preferredFinalMessageFromToolOrModel` now prefers a single successful tool's `userFacingText` over the evaluator's synthesized reply; Stage 1 retry logic is extended to catch malformed (present-but-empty) `HANDLE_RESPONSE` tool calls; canonical action names are registered before similes to prevent simile-based capture of real action surfaces.
- **Orchestrator + Discord** (`plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/index.ts`, `plugin-discord/service.ts`): Sub-agent progress delivery is gated by a configurable delay and mode policy, clearing timers on `task_complete`/`task_error`; Discord reply references are now propagated through `originConnectorMessageId` so final bot messages reply to the triggering prompt; `sub-agent-router.ts` adds verify-retry event suppression and makes `requireFresh` opt-in so files pre-dating a session start are no longer treated as dead URLs.
- **Infrastructure** (`task-drain.ts`, `batcher.ts`, `dispatcher.ts`, `plugin-sql/base.ts`): Duplicate persisted drain tasks are deleted on startup; recurring batcher sections are throttled by `minCycleMs`; `agentId` is added to `getTasksByName`/`getTask` return shapes needed by `matchesTask`.

<h3>Confidence Score: 4/5</h3>

Safe to merge with minor cleanup; all changes are well-tested and address real live-trajectory issues observed in production.

The three flagged issues are all non-blocking: a dead `chunks.length === 1` condition in the Discord multi-chunk path (doesn't affect the final reply behavior), a `shouldRun` predicate evaluated after the recurring-section timestamp is already advanced (potential over-suppression in niche section configs), and a redundant `typeof serialized === "string"` guard in `isUnsupportedCancelError`. None introduce incorrect behavior in the primary live paths. The core behavioral changes — Stage 1 retry extension, tool-text priority, action-tiering canonical-first registration, delay-based progress gating, and verify-retry suppression — are all clearly intentional, consistently tested, and address concrete trajectory failures observed in live runs.

`plugins/plugin-discord/service.ts` for the dead condition in the multi-chunk send path; `packages/core/src/utils/prompt-batcher/batcher.ts` for the mark-before-shouldRun ordering.

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>




| Filename | Overview |
|----------|----------|
| packages/core/src/runtime/planner-loop.ts | Adds `preferredFinalMessageFromToolOrModel` that gives a single successful tool's `userFacingText` priority over the evaluator's paraphrase to prevent hallucination of exact paths/values; logic is correct and well-covered by the new happy-path test. |
| packages/core/src/services/message.ts | Extends the Stage 1 empty-completion retry to also detect and retry malformed `HANDLE_RESPONSE` tool calls (tool present but arguments unparseable); retry limit and logging are correctly updated, and the new test covers the recovery path. |
| packages/core/src/features/autonomy/service.ts | Restricts broad participant-room scanning to an explicit opt-in flag (`AUTONOMY_INCLUDE_ALL_ROOMS=true`), preventing inadvertent room enumeration; hoists autonomy memory fetch before the room-ID check so thought context is always included. |
| plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/index.ts | Large addition of configurable progress policy (`compact`/`threaded`/`silent`) and a timer-based delay mechanism that suppresses early sub-agent progress noise; delay logic is largely correct but a narrow race where the timer fires just as `task_complete` is processed can emit a stale progress message after the completion one. |
| plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/services/sub-agent-router.ts | Adds session-event suppression after a verify-retry handoff and filters internal `session/cancel` MCP errors; changes `requireFresh` from opt-out (`!== false`) to opt-in (`=== true`), intentionally allowing pre-session-start files to pass verification, which the new stale-mtime test validates. |
| plugins/plugin-discord/service.ts | Adds Discord reply-reference propagation via `discordReplyReferenceFromContent`; single-chunk path is correct and tested, but the multi-chunk path contains a dead `chunks.length === 1` condition that can never be true inside `if (chunks.length > 1)`. |
| packages/core/src/utils/batch-queue/task-drain.ts | Deduplicates stale persisted drain tasks on startup by deleting all but the first matching task; uses `Promise.allSettled` so a single delete failure won't abort the rest. |
| packages/core/src/utils/prompt-batcher/batcher.ts | Adds `minCycleMs` throttle for recurring sections via `_markRecurringSectionAttempt`; timestamp is marked before `shouldRun` is evaluated, which could cause over-suppression when `shouldRun` gates a section that becomes ready within the cycle window. |
| plugins/plugin-sql/src/base.ts | Adds the missing `agentId` field to `getTasksByName` and `getTask` return mappings, needed by `TaskDrain.matchesTask` to filter tasks by agent; simple and targeted fix. |
| packages/core/src/utils/prompt-batcher/dispatcher.ts | Passes the maximum `maxRetries` across a call-plan's sections into the model invocation options; straightforward and well-tested by the new dispatcher test. |
| packages/core/src/runtime/action-tiering.ts | Refactors candidate matching to build a canonical-owners map so real action names take priority over simile-based matches, preventing a simile of `TASKS` from capturing the `TASKS` action surface. |
| packages/core/src/features/basic-capabilities/providers/attachments.ts | Skips attachment prompt rendering for `sub_agent` source messages, preventing stale sub-agent progress attachments from leaking into subsequent prompt context. |

</details>



<h3>Sequence Diagram</h3>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant U as User (Discord)
    participant DS as DiscordService
    participant MH as MessageHandler (Stage1)
    participant PL as PlannerLoop
    participant OR as OrchestratorPlugin
    participant SA as SubAgent (OpenCode)
    participant SR as SubAgentRouter

    U->>DS: Send prompt message
    DS->>MH: runV5MessageRuntimeStage1()
    MH-->>MH: Stage1 retry if empty/malformed tool call
    MH->>PL: planned_reply (TASKS action)
    PL->>OR: TASKS action handler
    OR->>SA: spawn sub-agent session
    OR-->>DS: (no progress yet, within delayMs)
    SA-->>OR: progress events
    OR-->>DS: emitProgress after delayMs (compact text)
    SA-->>SR: task_complete event
    SR->>SR: verifyMappedLocalUrl (requireFresh opt-in)
    SR-->>OR: clearDelayedProgress(sessionId)
    Note over PL: Single tool userFacingText wins over evaluator paraphrase
    PL->>DS: finalMessage with originConnectorMessageId
    DS->>U: Reply referencing original prompt message
```

<details><summary><h3>Comments Outside Diff (1)</h3></summary>

1. `packages/core/src/utils/prompt-batcher/batcher.ts`, line 698-710 ([link](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/blob/4ba5130529b67ab5c621281f6aa397fe106759f4/packages/core/src/utils/prompt-batcher/batcher.ts#L698-L710)) 

   <a href="#"><img alt="P2" src="https://greptile-static-assets.s3.amazonaws.com/badges/p2.svg?v=8" align="top"></a> `_markRecurringSectionAttempt` is called before the `shouldRun` predicate is evaluated. If `shouldRun` returns `false` (e.g., "no pending work yet"), the timestamp is still advanced, so the section will be suppressed for the full `minCycleMs` window even though it never produced any model output. For sections that combine `shouldRun` with a long `minCycleMs`, this can delay the first actual execution if the predicate flips to `true` shortly after a skipped cycle.

</details>

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<details><summary><h3>Greptile Summary</h3></summary>

This PR adds a Swift-specific RPC bridge (`POST /api/swift/rpc`) that maps named method envelopes to existing elizaOS REST routes, migrates the macOS client to use it exclusively, and implements a full native chat flow with conversation lifecycle management, queued prompts, and a runtime-readiness polling loop.

- **RPC bridge** (`swift-mac-rpc-routes.ts`): validates method names against a static allowlist, translates params to existing route calls, and forwards the upstream response in a typed `{ok, status, result}` envelope; the upstream URL is constructed from the incoming `Host` header, creating an SSRF exposure.
- **RuntimeController** (`RuntimeController.swift`): adds pipe-based stdout/stderr capture and a synchronous preflight script that blocks the `@MainActor` thread until the node process exits, which will freeze the UI.
- **AppModel** (`AppModel.swift`): implements queued chat, conversation lifecycle, and a 30-second readiness polling loop; contains a private helper `refreshRuntimeSetupSnapshot(after:)` that is defined but never called.
</details>


<h3>Confidence Score: 3/5</h3>

Two issues need to be resolved before merging: the RPC bridge builds its upstream URL from the untrusted Host header (SSRF), and the preflight script blocks the main actor thread synchronously, freezing the macOS UI on first launch.

The RPC bridge introduces an SSRF path where a forged Host header redirects proxy calls to arbitrary addresses. Separately, the synchronous waitUntilExit() call inside @MainActor-isolated ensureRuntimePreflight will stall all UI updates for however long the node code-generation script takes to run. Both are present defects on code paths that execute on every cold start and every RPC call.

swift-mac-rpc-routes.ts (SSRF in forwardRPCRequest) and RuntimeController.swift (blocking main actor in ensureRuntimePreflight) are the two files that need changes before this is safe to ship.

<details open><summary><h3>Security Review</h3></summary>

- **SSRF via `Host` header** (`packages/agent/src/api/swift-mac-rpc-routes.ts`, `forwardRPCRequest`): The upstream URL is assembled from the untrusted `Host` request header. A request with a crafted header (e.g., `Host: 169.254.169.254`) causes the bridge to proxy to that address. Fix: hardcode `127.0.0.1` and derive the port from the server socket.
</details>


<details><summary><h3>Important Files Changed</h3></summary>




| Filename | Overview |

| packages/agent/src/api/swift-mac-rpc-routes.ts | New RPC bridge forwarding Swift method names to existing elizaOS routes; contains an SSRF vulnerability where the upstream URL is built from the untrusted Host request header. |

| packages/app-core/platforms/macos/Sources/ElizaMacCore/Services/RuntimeController.swift | Adds pipe-based stdout/stderr capture and a preflight script runner; ensureRuntimePreflight calls waitUntilExit() synchronously on the @MainActor thread, which will freeze the UI for the duration of the node script. |
| packages/app-core/platforms/macos/Sources/ElizaMacCore/Services/RuntimeAPIClient.swift | All API calls now route through /api/swift/rpc; fetchSnapshot changed from fully concurrent to sequential health-first fetch, and a single 60-second timeout applies to all RPC calls including polling health checks. |
| packages/app-core/platforms/macos/Sources/ElizaMac/Stores/AppModel.swift | Adds full native chat flow and setup-snapshot refresh; contains unreachable refreshRuntimeSetupSnapshot(after:) private helper. |
| packages/app-core/platforms/macos/Sources/ElizaMacCore/Models/RuntimeTelemetry.swift | Adds conversation, permissions, and trade-mode model types; RuntimePermissionsSnapshot uses a custom Decodable with DynamicCodingKey to separate metadata keys from permission entries correctly. |
| packages/app-core/platforms/macos/Tests/ElizaMacCoreTests/RuntimeAPIClientTests.swift | Test suite updated to stub on RPC method names instead of paths; new tests cover permissions, conversation create, and message send flows with body content assertions. |
